## NOTES

### Sources and their weight

**`用廃機ハンターが行く！` (wrecks.hatenablog.com) carried this entire pass.** It is not a directory; it is a
national register kept by one census-taker ("Unikun") who visits sites, reads data plates and radio-call
placards, tracks Google Earth Pro imagery date by date, and reads Ministry of Defense procurement and
scrapping notices. Every article carries an explicit `<編集履歴>` revision log, a `<訪問日>` list of visit
dates, and usually a `<座標>` coordinate. The structure is doubled: **248 per-site articles** (organised by
prefecture) and **~90 per-type national censuses** (F-86F, F-104J base/civil split, T-33A, T-34A, T-6, T-1,
T-2, T-3, T-4, F-1, F-4, C-46, C-1, S-62, V-107, Nike-J, and for the JGSDF UH-1B/H/J, OH-6J/D, V-107A,
TH-55J, LR-1, L-19, AH-1S, CH-47J). Cross-reading the two axes is what makes the data checkable: a site
article and a type census have to agree on a serial or one of them is wrong, and the author repeatedly
flags where they don't.

Several articles were revised within weeks of this pass: Komatsu (17 Aug 2026, display line relocated),
Matsumoto camp (20-22 Apr 2026, visited 19 Apr 2026), Nihon Wellness Sports University (01 Jun 2026,
visited 23 May 2026), Yukari Traffic Park (10 Sep 2026 — removal notice), UH-1H census (23 Jul 2026),
OH-6D census (08 Jun 2026). The most recent site visit used anywhere in this file is **19-23 April 2026**.

**OpenStreetMap Overpass sweep** — `historic=aircraft`, `memorial=aircraft`, `memorial:type=aircraft` and
`aeroway=gate_guardian`, run as four bbox queries (Hokkaido / Honshu / Shikoku-Kyushu / Okinawa) through
the **maps.mail.ru** mirror; overpass-api.de is blocked here and `area["ISO3166-1"="JP"]` returns nothing,
exactly as the brief warned. Yield: **101 nodes**, almost all of them corroboration rather than new leads.
It independently confirmed: the Nakatombetsu F-104J serial (`ref=46-8568`), three Hofu-Kita serials in
`inscription` tags (04-8203, 15-5816, 51-5632), the Kamagaya SNJ number 6209, exactly four aircraft
memorials at Naha, the Miho SE display park, and the Michi-no-Eki Higashi-Matsushima T-4. It produced
**one genuinely new lead**: a sawn-off aircraft used as a shop sign in Kyoto (`KYOTO OLYMPIA`, 35.0059N
135.7671E), which appears in no Japanese source and is recorded here as an unidentified airframe.

Deliberately not used: aviationmuseum.eu and silverhawkauthor.com (lead lists, and neither has meaningful
Japanese coverage); Grokipedia. Japanese Wikipedia was checked and, as the brief said, has **no per-type
保存機 sections** for the JASDF jets — no time was spent there.

### Corrections made

- **Lake Hamana C-47**: the source header prints the longitude as `37.6165E`, which is in the Yellow Sea.
  Corrected to 137.6165E from the site description.
- **Nihon Aviation Academy Yamanashi**: the source prints several longitudes as `38.48xx` (dropped leading
  1). Corrected to 138.48xx.
- **Nihon Wellness Sports University**: the site article's `<座標>` header repeats `36.3623N / 140.3574E`,
  which is the Mito Uchihara Health Park entry from a different article. The coordinate used here
  (35.8593N 140.1460E) is the one carried in the UH-1J and OH-6D censuses.
- **Kokusai Aviation College** and **Higashi-Nihon Aviation College**: both site articles carry a stray
  Miyagi/Ibaraki address block copied from another entry. Addresses taken from the body text; the bad
  coordinate is omitted rather than propagated.
- **Kyoto City Citizens' Disaster Prevention Centre**: the article header carries a Korean address from
  another entry; ignored.
- **Kazuma station**: the source prints `14.1905E`; the real value is 141.1905E, confirmed by the OSM node.
- **Shiobara Family Farm**: source longitude reads `139.989` with no final digit; recorded as given and
  flagged rather than invented.
- **Matsushima F-86F 82-7789**: the standard reference (Hikoki-gumo) gives the withdrawal date and last
  unit for 92-7889 by copying the line for 92-7888. The blog identifies this as a copy-paste error and the
  description records the real airframe rather than the propagated one.
- **Yokota F-86F**: recorded as 62-7511 with 62-7517 in aliases, and the reason for the ambiguity (a
  handwritten 1 vs 7 in the original ledger) stated in the description, rather than silently picking one.
- **Kadena T-33A 53-5612**: recorded with the serial as published but with the dispute stated — that same
  serial belongs to an aircraft displayed at Shizuhama and later scrapped.
- **Naha B-65 03-3095**: the leading `0` is 1980, not 1970 — this airframe was returned from the JMSDF
  (as 6728) to the JASDF on 5 Mar 1980. A good illustration of why the fiscal-year prefix is never a build
  year.
- **Ibaraki RF-4EJ 87-6412**: recorded with 87-8412 in aliases because the serial was deliberately changed
  on conversion (JASDF digit 8 = fighter, 6 = reconnaissance).

### Painted markings that are NOT the identity

Japan repaints display airframes constantly. Every one of these is recorded with the true serial in
`tail_number`, the worn marking in `aliases`, and the reason in the description:

| Site | True identity | Wears |
|---|---|---|
| Camp Kita-Utsunomiya | F-86F 92-7883 | 82-7818 (Blue Impulse) |
| Gifu Air Base | F-86F 92-7897 | 62-7427 |
| Koda Local History Museum | F-86F 92-7910 | 72-7743 |
| Misawa (HQ) | F-86F 62-7508 | 55-7508 right side / 3925 left side |
| Iruma | F-104J 46-8561 | 56-8666 |
| Nyutabaru | F-104J 46-8656 | 36-8535 |
| Metase-no-Mori | T-33A 91-5403 | 81-5358 |
| Camp Naha | LR-1 22016 | 22019 |
| Komatsushima | SH-60J 8273 | 8224 |
| Nyutabaru | T-2 89-5196 | 69-5127 (aggressor scheme) |
| Kadena | F-4C 64-0913 | 37433 / 63-7433 |
| Atsugi | A-4E Bu.151074 | Bu.150122 |
| Nippon Bunri University | F-86F 62-7437 | nose 437 / 62-7473 / 92-7880 all on one airframe |
| Chofu Propeller Cafe | Cessna 150B JA3171 | JA418 |
| Ashiya | H-19C 91-4707 | 91-4777 |
| Minobu | F-86F 12-7996 | USAF 12834 / FU-834 |

### Composites and part-airframes

Tail-boom and panel swaps are endemic among JGSDF helicopters and are recorded in the description with the
donor serial in `aliases`: OH-6D 31178 (boom from 31201, Hachinohe), 31136 (from 31186, Yamato), 31195
(from 31218, Kiryu), 31246 (from 31224, Kokubu), 31197 (from 31214, KIT), 31245 (from 31273, Piso Tenjin),
31180 (from 31174, Yamanashi); UH-1H 41644 and 41721 at Narashino have swapped nose panels, so each wears
the other's number. T-1A 85-5803 at Ito Denki is a genuine two-airframe composite (rear fuselage 85-5802).
V-107A 51803 at Minobu is a JGSDF fuselage with a JASDF KV-107 rotor head. F-104J 76-8688 at Naha has the
rear fuselage of 76-8683. Nose-only and cockpit-only exhibits are recorded as records (the contract's
"deliberate retention plus public presentation" test) with the extent stated in the description.

### Judgment calls

- **`region` = `Asia`** on every row, per the brief's override.
- **Base displays are `restricted`, not `public`**, unless the aircraft is genuinely visible from a public
  road without entering. Sites moved to `public` on that basis: Iruma (southern perimeter road), Fuchu,
  Camp Otsu, Camp Matsudo, Camp Itazuma, Camp Jinmachi's equipment park (open weekdays 09:00-16:00 with no
  booking), Rikkun Land.
- **JGSDF "訓練機" (training airframes)** — boarding-drill and maintenance-instruction hulks parked on camp
  grounds — are included where the census records them as deliberately retained and viewable on open days,
  but every one says so in its description and several are `in_storage` rather than `on_display`. This is
  the single biggest judgment call in the file. If the database wants only plinthed exhibits, the rows
  whose descriptions say "training airframe" are the ones to drop; they are all flagged.
- **Replicas flagged**: the Iwakuni A6M2 (built for the film *Zero-sen Moyu*), the Ashiya Ki-43 eight-tenths
  model, the Komatsu F-15 "fin monument" (a shape, not an airframe). Fin-only monuments (Misawa F-4EJ Kai
  27-8305, Kumagaya F-1 10-8255, Matsushima T-4 26-5804 and 46-5725, Tsuiki and Naha F-15J, Kasuga F-4EJ Kai
  57-8353, Nobeoka T-33A 91-5406) are recorded as records with "fin only" in the description.
- **Nike-J surface-to-air missiles** are recorded as `missile_rocket` / `surface_to_air`, manufacturer
  Mitsubishi (licence-built Nike Hercules). The national Nike-J census lists 24 sites; only those attached
  to a site already in this file are given rows, plus the Kadena liquor-shop advertising missile.
- **`operator_country`**: `JP` for JASDF/JMSDF/JGSDF and for ex-SDF airframes now in civilian hands; `US`
  for USAF/USN/USMC machines at Misawa, Yokota, Atsugi, Iwakuni and Kadena, for the loaned A-4E at Clinic
  Shakura, and for the US-registered trainers at Osaka Aviation College and the YS-11 N462AL; `TH` for the
  ex-Royal Thai Air Force C-47 at Lake Hamana. Left **blank** for the Kadena F-86F (disputed JASDF vs ROKAF
  origin), the OSM-only Kyoto shop-sign aircraft, and the two unidentified Sendai entries.
- **Manufacturer attribution for licence-built types**: F-86F serials ending 401-535 are MAP-supplied and
  credited to North American; 701-999 and 0xx are Mitsubishi-built. T-33A serials ending 601-668 are
  Lockheed (MAP); 201-410 are Kawasaki. F-104J = Mitsubishi, F-104DJ = Lockheed. F-4EJ/RF-4EJ = Mitsubishi,
  RF-4E = McDonnell Douglas. This rule is stated here so a later pass can audit it.
- **`year_built` is blank on every row.** JASDF serials are fiscal-year contract prefixes, exactly like a US
  FY block, and the blog gives acceptance and withdrawal dates but very rarely a construction date. The two
  places where a real build date exists (Uji UH-1H 41659, December 1979; Hagi KM-2 6290, 12 Nov 1982) are
  stated in the description rather than guessed into the field.
- **Sites also in the parallel museums pass**: Daikeien, Hijiri Museum and Mitsu Seiki are duplicated here
  deliberately, because they read as arcade / village-monument / company-garden sites rather than museums
  and a de-duplicating import should keep one copy. Hamamatsu Air Base is a separate record from Hamamatsu
  Air Park; Kanoya base perimeter has no separate display outside the museum, so no record was made.

### EXCLUDED, and why

**Removed or scrapped since the last survey — do not re-research these.** All are documented with dates:

- *Scrapped in place*: Tateyama JMSDF (B-65 6726, KM-2 6259, HSS-2B 8085, S-61A-1 8185, SH-60J 8221 — all
  11-15 Mar 2024, the site is now empty); Ominato JMSDF gate (HSS-2B 8167 and SH-60J 8249, both gone by
  Jun 2026); Nanatsuto Central Park HSS-2B 8165 (Oct 2025); Yura JMSDF HSS-2B 8105 (by Mar 2021);
  Suo-Oshima Nagisa Park PS-1 5818 (15-16 Nov 2021); Saijo Shirakawa F-104DJ 46-5008 (Feb 2024);
  Kyoyama Solar Green Park F-104J 76-8687 (by Nov 2025); Kumagaya F-104J 76-8704 and T-6G 52-0128 (2021);
  Ashiya T-33A 51-5610 and F-104J 56-8663 (23 Aug 2021); Hyakuri F-4EJ Kai 17-8302 and RF-4E 57-6906
  (17 Jan 2022); Chitose F-104J 76-8689 (2020), T-33A 71-5303 and T-34A 71-0416; Okadama V-107A 51703
  (by Nov 2025); Onagawa/Gozensaki T-3 11-5539 (Mar 2025); Bihoro Aviation Park V-44A, T-6G 52-0076,
  T-34A 60505, UH-1B 41583 (13 Dec 2016); Camp Matsudo C-1 fatigue-test fuselage (29 Mar 2021) and
  V-107 51737 (30 Mar 2021); Narashino C-1 static-test fuselage (Mar 2026); Mitsubishi Komaki South F-86F
  62-7702, F-104J 56-8672 and HSS-2B 8084 (2025); Kita-Kumamoto UH-1H 41646 (Feb 2025) and OH-6D 31132
  (Mar 2024); Shizuoka Institute of Science and Technology LR-1 22010, OH-6D 31153, Bell 222 JA9552 (2022);
  Narita College UH-1H 41640, OH-6J 31075, LR-1 22007, two TH-55J, H-13KH 30219.
- *Closed or dispersed collections*: Old Car Center Kudan, Naraha, Fukushima — held fifteen airframes
  including F-104J 76-8705, F-104DJ 26-5001 and 26-5005, RF-4EJ 57-6376, T-2 69-5117, TH-55J 61307,
  L-19E 11214; **all removed by March 2025**, most to a private collector in Kitakyushu. Kissa Hikojo cafe,
  Hamamatsu — eight airframes, shop and aircraft both gone (last two removed 10 Jun 2022). Onraku-kan,
  Asakura (closed 2022, KM-2 6260 and 6272 went to auction). Ruri-kei Zero-sen Kenkyujo (building
  demolished before Aug 2024). Sayama Yuen, Osaka (record only). Nagano City Youth Science Centre (closed
  Mar 2022, OH-6J 31084 returned and scrapped). Sapporo Youth Science Museum (transferred).
- *Removed civic monuments*: Mito Uchihara Health Park UH-1H 41621 (Dec 2022); Omitama Ogawa Marine Centre
  UH-1H 41648 (Jan 2023); Funabashi Misaki Kobato nursery OH-6D 31230; Ninki-no-Sato Park, Eiheiji OH-6D
  31139 (2022); Kirishima Maruoka Park OH-6D 31211 (moved to Camp Kokubu); Hakushu nursery T-34A 71-0430
  (2025); Green Springs Tachikawa R-HM (moved to Olympos); Tochigi Children's Science Museum (aviation
  exhibits removed); Seikodo, Uonuma F-86F (2025); Tottori airport YS-11 JA8662 fuselage; Yokohama Midori-ku
  T-33A 71-5313 (moved to a private site in Ibaraki, 6 Jul 2024); Takamatsu Route 11 UH-1B (moved to the new
  Tenpos store and recorded there instead).
- *Not display records under the contract's test*: private collections the census itself refuses to count
  because they cannot be seen from any public place (T-1B 25-5852, 25-5855, 25-5857; T-33A 81-5375,
  71-5313; an F-4EJ nose in a private collection; the Akita T-33A offered on Yahoo Auctions). Aircraft on
  active flightlines and IRAN/maintenance airframes at Komaki and Miho that were mistaken online for
  withdrawn machines. Kadena's F-15A/F-100A/F-105F are included, but only because they were shipped in
  purely to be displayed.
- *Well-searched zeroes*: **there is no JMSDF display aircraft at Hachinohe, Atsugi, Iwakuni (JMSDF side
  proper) or Naha** — the national JMSDF census says so explicitly. **Misawa is the only JMSDF-adjacent
  Aomori site with aircraft, and they are at the prefectural museum, not the base.** The brief's list named
  Kanoya perimeter, Ominato, Hachinohe, Tateyama, Omura, Shimofusa, Iwakuni and Atsugi as JMSDF targets:
  of those, **Ominato and Tateyama are now empty**, **Hachinohe and Omura have nothing displayed**, and
  Kanoya's aircraft are all inside the museum the parallel pass covered. Likewise **Akeno, Metabaru and
  Takayubaru** — three of the brief's JGSDF targets — produced **no current display airframes**: Akeno's
  UH-1H 41633 is long gone, Metabaru's TH-55J 61317 and OH-6J 31017 were removed before 2010, and
  Takayubaru's only current aircraft is OH-6D 31247, which is a travelling exhibit kept by the Western Army
  field maintenance unit rather than a fixed display. **Sasebo** has nothing: the B-65 6723 and an
  unidentified aircraft at the JMSDF guard unit compound were gone after 2013.

### Blank fields, deliberately

- `year_built` — blank throughout; see the judgment-calls note above.
- `postal_code` — filled only where the source prints a 〒 code; most camp and park articles do not.
- `website` — almost always blank; the census links to unit pages, not to site pages, and inventing one
  would be worse than none.
- `latitude`/`longitude` — blank where the source **deliberately withholds** the position (Seto private
  residence, Kadena-cho private residence, Hokota private residence, Kasama scrap yard given only to three
  decimals) or simply never gives one (Shinkiba C-46, Aozora Hustle, Mitsuwa Hagane, Hokuryo High School,
  Chiba Institute of Science, Kanazawa Institute of Technology, Teikyo University, Tachiarai station,
  Higashi-Nihon Aviation College). Guessing them from a town name would be worse than a blank.
- `tail_number` — blank where no serial has ever been read: the Kadena F-86F, the Kakogawa rooftop F-86F,
  the Izumo and Ainoura and Higashi-Chitose UH-1Hs, the Hokota T-34A, the Tokushima TC-90, the Ikiiki
  Shinsui E18S, the Sabage Paradise SH-60J.
- `aircraft_name` — blank everywhere; Japanese display airframes do not carry individual names.

### Needs a human — ranked

1. **Yukari Traffic Park, Matsudo — S-62 removal, early October 2026.** A bulletin dated 10 Sep 2026 says
   removal is scheduled for early October. This is the single most time-critical row in the file. Matsudo
   City Parks and Green Space: the park is at 1-25 Koganehara, Matsudo. Verify before import whether the
   record should be `on_display` or historic.
2. **Kaizuka Traffic Park, Fukuoka — DH.114 Heron JA6159.** Display stated to end 31 Mar 2027; a new keeper
   was advertised in Mar 2026 and a destination announced 30 Aug 2026. This is the last Heron in Japan and
   the destination needs confirming so the site record follows the airframe.
3. **Tsuiki and Izumo scrapping notices, July 2026.** wrecks.hatenablog.com/entry/2026/07/12/105916 covers
   two disposal notices — a Camp Izumo OH-6J and a Tsuiki airframe not yet identified in the article. One
   call to each base PR section would settle both.
4. **Ibaraki Airport Phantoms.** An Aug 2026 report describes a planned new exchange facility in front of
   the airport and raises the possibility of the two Phantoms moving. Ibaraki Airport, Omitama.
5. **The five sites with no coordinate and no visit**: Shinkiba C-46 61-1130 (Koto-ku, Tokyo), Aozora
   Hustle airsoft field T-6D 52-0001 (Toyokawa, Aichi), Mitsuwa Hagane F-104J 36-8538 nose and T-33A
   91-5406 fin (Nobeoka, Miyazaki), Hokuryo High School OH-6D 31261 (Saga), Chiba Institute of Science
   OH-6D 31163 (Choshi). Each is a single phone call or one visit.
6. **Sendai City Science Museum and Sendai Airport.** Both articles record aircraft after 2024/2026 visits
   but neither published extract itemises them. Two `Unidentified` rows are standing in.
7. **Kyoto Olympia shop-sign aircraft** (35.0059N 135.7671E). OSM-only, uncorroborated, type unknown. It is
   the one lead in this pass that no Japanese source knows about.
8. **Nihon Aviation Academy Ishikawa (Noto)** — nine airframes including four YS-11s. The campus was hit by
   the 1 Jan 2024 Noto earthquake; the aircraft are reported undamaged but the school relocated temporarily.
   Current status and access need confirming.
9. **Chiba Institute of Science, Choshi** — the blog flags a Sankei report of 28 Nov 2023 that the
   institution may not continue and advises seeing the OH-6D early.
10. **SUBARU Utsunomiya South Plant AH-1S 73403.** The census article title itself ends in a question mark.
    Company premises, no visit ever made.
